People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I, um... I guess I'm just here because I've been feeling a little down lately. It's probably nothing major, I mean, I'm sure you see people with much bigger problems. But I just... I keep having these thoughts that I've completely let my family down. We took on this mortgage recently, and I just feel like it was a mistake and it's all my fault. And then at work—I'm a waiter—every little thing I mess up, even just dropping a fork, it just makes me feel like I'm completely useless. Like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me. Um, well... I'm not sure if it's a big deal, but I guess some people at work have mentioned a few things. I really try to keep up, I promise I do, but... sometimes I get kind of stuck? Um... well, I... I don't want you to think I'm crazy or anything, because I know it probably sounds really weird. And I'm sure it's just my imagination, honestly. But... sometimes, yeah. 

Mostly it's just when it's really quiet. Like if I'm closing up the restaurant late at night, or if I'm just sitting by myself in my condo. I'll hear this... this low voice. It just says my name, or sometimes it whispers that I'm going to lose everything. It sounds so real that I actually turn around to see who's there, but nobody ever is. 

I try to just ignore it, you know? I don't want to make a big deal out of it. It's probably just stress from work, right? I mean, a lot of people probably hear things when they're tired... is that something you hear a lot from people? I'm sorry if that's a weird answer.

Um... well, it's... it's hard to explain, I guess. I really want to say it's just my own thoughts, you know? Like maybe I'm just being too hard on myself and my brain is playing tricks on me because I'm so worried about doing a good job. 

But... when I'm closing up the restaurant or just sitting alone in my condo, that low voice... it really does sound like it's coming from outside of my head. Um... well, I wouldn't say a significant amount, no. I really try to take good care of myself, especially since I need to look presentable for work. But... my eating has been a little bit... all over the place lately, I guess.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh... um... no. No, I would never do anything to actually hurt myself. I promise. I know that's a really serious thing, and I would definitely tell you if I was planning anything like that. I really don't want you to worry about me being a danger to myself or anything.

But... well, sometimes... and I really hope you don't think less of me for saying this... sometimes when I'm lying awake in the condo, especially right before I have to get up for an early shift, things just feel really heavy. And I'll catch myself thinking that... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Just so I wouldn't have to keep trying so hard and feeling like I'm failing at everything. 

It's just a passing thought, though! It's not a plan or anything, I swear. I just get so tired of feeling like I'm messing up. Is it... is it normal to feel like that sometimes? I hope I'm not oversharing or making it sound worse than it is... I just want to be completely honest with you so you can help me.

Oh, no, definitely not. The voice doesn't give me any secret codes or special messages, I promise. I know that would be a really bad sign, and I swear I don't think I'm special or have powers or anything like that. 

But... um... since you're asking about feeling like things are directed at me... there is something else that's been bothering me. I'm a little nervous to bring it up, because I really don't want to sound paranoid or crazy to you. You won't judge me, right? 

It's just... I can't seem to shake this feeling lately that the bank, and sometimes even my manager at the restaurant, are specifically watching me. Like they're just waiting for me to mess up. Even when something happens and people show me the proof that it was just a completely normal paperwork mistake or a regular register error... I still get this awful feeling that they're secretly keeping track and targeting me. 

I really try not to let it affect how I treat my customers, though! I always smile and do my best to be a good employee. I just... I don't know why my brain does that. Does that kind of answer your question, even though it's not about the voice? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well... I wouldn't use the word *overwhelming*, exactly. I mean, I never just walk out or break down at work or anything. I always make sure to finish my shifts and take care of my tables, I promise. I really try to keep it together.

But... I guess... if I'm being completely honest... during those really bad stretches when I'm feeling the most down and heavy? The other stuff doesn't go away. The voice, and that awful feeling that my manager or the bank is watching me... they're still right there. 

So I end up having to deal with both things at the exact same time, just trying to push through and get through my shifts without anyone noticing. It gets a little exhausting trying to juggle it all while remembering everyone's orders, but I really do try my hardest not to let it affect my service. 

I hope you don't think I'm making excuses or just complaining... I know everybody has hard days at work. I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right. Did that make sense, or did you mean something else?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well... I guess I've noticed a little bit of a change there. But I really try not to let it affect my work! I used to always joke around with the regulars, you know, really chat them up. And I'd always text my brother right after my shifts to catch up. 

Lately, though... I guess I just haven't had the same energy for it. I mostly just keep my face straight at work now and only say what I absolutely have to say to get the orders right. And with my brother... I just let his messages sit there for a while. It's not that I don't care about him! I just... I don't really feel that pull to answer right away anymore. 

I feel terrible about it, honestly. I know I should be more engaging, and I promise I'm trying to get back to being that fun, outgoing person. I don't want anyone to think I'm ignoring them on purpose or being rude. I'm just... trying to conserve my energy, I guess? Does that make sense? I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for.

But even during those good weeks, when the heavy, guilty feelings lifted... the other stuff didn't stop. I still heard that whispering when I was at the condo. And I still had that awful feeling that I was being watched, especially over my mortgage paperwork. \n\nI really tried my best to just focus on my good mood and push past the whispering and the paperwork stuff, though! I didn't let it ruin those better weeks. Is that... does that help explain things? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right details and answering what you actually asked.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy doing things that you used to find pleasurable, like hobbies or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess I've noticed a little bit of a change there. But I really try not to let it affect my work! I used to always joke around with the regulars, you know, really chat them up. And I'd always text my brother right after my shifts to catch up. \n\nLately, though... I guess I just haven't had the same energy for it. I mostly just keep my face straight at work now and only say what I absolutely have to say to get the orders right. And with my brother... I just let his messages sit there for a while. It's not that I don't care about him! I just... I don't really feel that pull to answer right away anymore. \n\nI feel terrible about it, honestly. I know I should be more engaging, and I promise I'm trying to get back to being that fun, outgoing person. I don't want anyone to think I'm ignoring them on purpose or being rude. I'm just... trying to conserve my energy, I guess? Does that make sense? I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks where you felt completely free of these depressed and guilty feelings?\n\nPatient: Um... I think we might have just talked about this a little bit, but I want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need. \n\nSo, yes, um... there was that time, like I mentioned, where for several weeks my mood wasn't really depressed at all. I was getting through work so much better, and I was really trying to hold onto that good feeling, I promise. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, yes, actually. I know we sort of just touched on this, but... yes. 

There was that stretch of several weeks where I wasn't really feeling down or guilty at all. My mood was actually pretty good, and I was getting through my shifts at the restaurant a lot better. I was really proud of how well I was doing at work during that time. 

But... um... yeah, even during those good weeks, when I wasn't depressed, I still heard the whispering when I was at the condo. And I still had that feeling of being watched when I was dealing with my mortgage paperwork. 

I really tried to just ignore it and focus on the positive, though! I didn't want it to ruin the progress I was making with my mood. I hope it doesn't seem like I'm making things up or, you know, not trying hard enough to get better. I'm really doing my best to be a good patient. Does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

I'm not sure if it's a big deal, but I guess some people at work have mentioned a few things. I really try to keep up, I promise I do, but... sometimes I get kind of stuck? Like, I'll catch myself pacing around the restaurant, checking the same tables and receipts over and over again to make sure I didn't mess up. \n\nAnd then other times, it's like the opposite. I just feel so heavy and slow. Even just grabbing menus or walking over to the POS system takes me a lot longer than it usually would. I hate that it affects my work, and I'm really trying to be better about it. Do you think that's normal for when people feel down like this? I hope I'm explaining it right...\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I... I don't want you to think I'm crazy or anything, because I know it probably sounds really weird. And I'm sure it's just my imagination, honestly. But... sometimes, yeah. \n\nMostly it's just when it's really quiet. Like if I'm closing up the restaurant late at night, or if I'm just sitting by myself in my condo. I'll hear this... this low voice. It just says my name, or sometimes it whispers that I'm going to lose everything. It sounds so real that I actually turn around to see who's there, but nobody ever is. \n\nI try to just ignore it, you know? I don't want to make a big deal out of it. It's probably just stress from work, right? I mean, a lot of people probably hear things when they're tired... is that something you hear a lot from people? I'm sorry if that's a weird answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is real or part of your own thoughts?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... it's hard to explain, I guess. I really want to say it's just my own thoughts, you know? Like maybe I'm just being too hard on myself and my brain is playing tricks on me because I'm so worried about doing a good job. \n\nBut... when I'm closing up the restaurant or just sitting alone in my condo, that low voice... it really does sound like it's coming from outside of my head. Like, when it says my name or whispers that I'm going to lose everything, it sounds so real that I physically turn around to check if someone snuck in. \n\nI know that probably sounds bad, and I really hope you don't think I'm making it up for attention or anything. I just... I want to give you the right answer. Does that make sense? Like, even though I know logically there's no one there, in that exact second, it feels completely real to me. Is that... is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have the voices or whispers you've been hearing ever told you to do something, or given you any commands?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh, no. No, nothing like that, I promise. I know that would be really concerning, and I would definitely tell you if it did. \n\nLike I was saying just a minute ago, it really just sticks to those same things... mostly when I'm closing the restaurant or sitting by myself in the condo. It just says my name, or whispers that I'm going to lose everything. That's really all it does. Even though it sounds real enough that I still turn around to check the room, it never, ever tells me to actually do anything or gives me any orders. \n\nI wouldn't listen if it did, I swear. I really try to be a good person. Does that mean it's maybe not as severe, since it's not telling me to do things? I hope I'm doing a good job explaining it all to you... I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say a significant amount, no. I really try to take good care of myself, especially since I need to look presentable for work. But... my eating has been a little bit... all over the place lately, I guess. \n\nLike, some weeks I'm just so focused on doing a good job that I barely even touch the staff meal during my shift. I just don't really have much of an appetite. But then... and I'm a little embarrassed to admit this, so I really hope you don't judge me... other nights I'll get back to the condo and just eat whatever is in the kitchen. Just to sort of... feel settled, I guess? Like to calm down after a long day. \n\nSo it kind of balances out, I think. My weight might fluctuate a little, but I'm definitely not starving myself or anything, I promise. I hope that's an okay answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right details.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you were dead?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... no. No, I would never do anything to actually hurt myself. I promise. I know that's a really serious thing, and I would definitely tell you if I was planning anything like that. I really don't want you to worry about me being a danger to myself or anything.\n\nBut... well, sometimes... and I really hope you don't think less of me for saying this... sometimes when I'm lying awake in the condo, especially right before I have to get up for an early shift, things just feel really heavy. And I'll catch myself thinking that... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Just so I wouldn't have to keep trying so hard and feeling like I'm failing at everything. \n\nIt's just a passing thought, though! It's not a plan or anything, I swear. I just get so tired of feeling like I'm messing up. Is it... is it normal to feel like that sometimes? I hope I'm not oversharing or making it sound worse than it is... I just want to be completely honest with you so you can help me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like the voice you hear is giving you a message that only you can understand?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. The voice doesn't give me any secret codes or special messages, I promise. I know that would be a really bad sign, and I swear I don't think I'm special or have powers or anything like that. \n\nBut... um... since you're asking about feeling like things are directed at me... there is something else that's been bothering me. I'm a little nervous to bring it up, because I really don't want to sound paranoid or crazy to you. You won't judge me, right? \n\nIt's just... I can't seem to shake this feeling lately that the bank, and sometimes even my manager at the restaurant, are specifically watching me. Like they're just waiting for me to mess up. Even when something happens and people show me the proof that it was just a completely normal paperwork mistake or a regular register error... I still get this awful feeling that they're secretly keeping track and targeting me. \n\nI really try not to let it affect how I treat my customers, though! I always smile and do my best to be a good employee. I just... I don't know why my brain does that. Does that kind of answer your question, even though it's not about the voice? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these feelings of being down and guilty for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes. I guess when I really think about it... it has been exactly about that long. Like, a year and two months, give or take. \n\nI really try not to let it show, though! I always make sure to put on a smile for my tables and I try to be upbeat for my coworkers. I'd hate to be a downer or make anyone else's shift harder. But... yeah, underneath all that, those heavy, guilty feelings have kind of just been hanging around for that whole time. Almost every day. \n\nI feel a little embarrassed admitting it's been going on for over a year... I feel like I should have been able to just snap out of it by now, you know? I really hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself or not trying hard enough. I promise I'm trying my best to push through it. Does that give you the timeline you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where the voice you hear or the feelings of being watched were particularly intense or overwhelming?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I wouldn't use the word *overwhelming*, exactly. I mean, I never just walk out or break down at work or anything. I always make sure to finish my shifts and take care of my tables, I promise. I really try to keep it together.\n\nBut... I guess... if I'm being completely honest... during those really bad stretches when I'm feeling the most down and heavy? The other stuff doesn't go away. The voice, and that awful feeling that my manager or the bank is watching me... they're still right there. \n\nSo I end up having to deal with both things at the exact same time, just trying to push through and get through my shifts without anyone noticing. It gets a little exhausting trying to juggle it all while remembering everyone's orders, but I really do try my hardest not to let it affect my service. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm making excuses or just complaining... I know everybody has hard days at work. I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right. Did that make sense, or did you mean something else?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods over the past year and two months where you felt completely free of these down and guilty feelings?\n\nPatient: Um, actually, yes! There was... well, I want to say there was a stretch of several weeks where my mood wasn't really depressed at all. I was getting through work so much better, and I was really proud of myself for that. I was really trying hard to keep that positive momentum going, I promise. \n\nBut... um... I'm a little hesitant to say this, because I really don't want you to think I'm taking steps backward or not trying to get better. But even during those good weeks, when the heavy, guilty feelings lifted... the other stuff didn't stop. I still heard that whispering when I was at the condo. And I still had that awful feeling that I was being watched, especially over my mortgage paperwork. \n\nI really tried my best to just focus on my good mood and push past the whispering and the paperwork stuff, though! I didn't let it ruin those better weeks. Is that... does that help explain things? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right details and answering what you actually asked.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy doing things that you used to find pleasurable, like hobbies or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess I've noticed a little bit of a change there. But I really try not to let it affect my work! I used to always joke around with the regulars, you know, really chat them up. And I'd always text my brother right after my shifts to catch up. \n\nLately, though... I guess I just haven't had the same energy for it. I mostly just keep my face straight at work now and only say what I absolutely have to say to get the orders right. And with my brother... I just let his messages sit there for a while. It's not that I don't care about him! I just... I don't really feel that pull to answer right away anymore. \n\nI feel terrible about it, honestly. I know I should be more engaging, and I promise I'm trying to get back to being that fun, outgoing person. I don't want anyone to think I'm ignoring them on purpose or being rude. I'm just... trying to conserve my energy, I guess? Does that make sense? I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks where you felt completely free of these depressed and guilty feelings?\n\nPatient: Um... I think we might have just talked about this a little bit, but I want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need. \n\nSo, yes, um... there was that time, like I mentioned, where for several weeks my mood wasn't really depressed at all. I was getting through work so much better, and I was really trying to hold onto that good feeling, I promise. I was doing my best to be positive.\n\nBut... just to be completely honest, because I know you need all the details... even when the heavy feelings went away, the other things didn't. I was still hearing that whispering when I was at the condo. And I still had that awful, nervous feeling that I was being watched, especially when I was looking at my mortgage paperwork. \n\nI really hope it doesn't sound like I wasn't trying during those weeks. I was! I just... I couldn't make the whispering or the feeling of being watched stop, even when my mood was better. Is that... did I explain that right? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question properly since you asked again.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard the voice or felt like you were being watched when you were not feeling down or guilty?\n\nPatient: Um, yes, actually. I know we sort of just touched on this, but... yes. \n\nThere was that stretch of several weeks where I wasn't really feeling down or guilty at all. My mood was actually pretty good, and I was getting through my shifts at the restaurant a lot better. I was really proud of how well I was doing at work during that time. \n\nBut... um... yeah, even during those good weeks, when I wasn't depressed, I still heard the whispering when I was at the condo. And I still had that feeling of being watched when I was dealing with my mortgage paperwork. \n\nI really tried to just ignore it and focus on the positive, though! I didn't want it to ruin the progress I was making with my mood. I hope it doesn't seem like I'm making things up or, you know, not trying hard enough to get better. I'm really doing my best to be a good patient. Does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period of feeling down and guilty over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really don't want to sound like I'm just complaining or not trying hard enough to be happy. I really do try to focus on the positive things! But... if I'm looking at that whole timeline you mentioned, the past year and two months... I guess it hasn't really been just a couple of separate periods coming and going. \n\nTo be completely honest—because I know you need the full picture to help me—for most of this whole period... even when some days were a bit lighter and I could put on a good face at the restaurant, I’ve been weighed down by that empty, guilty feeling more often than not. \n\nI feel terrible admitting that, because I really am trying my hardest to shake it off and just be normal. I hope that answers your question okay? Um... well, yes, actually. I did have a stretch like that. I really hope this shows that I'm making some progress!
